length | 20-100, truncated exponential, scale 60, empirical mean 51 | \"Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials ... (empirical mean of 51 trials)\" |\n   186→| Block probabilities | 20:80 or 80:20 | \"at a ratio of 20:80% (right block) or 80:20% (left block)\" |\n   187→| Contrasts | 100, 25, 12.5, 6.25, 0 % in ratio 2:2:2:2:1 | \"Stimulus contrast was uniformly sampled from 5 possible values\" |\n   188→| **Reaction times < 80 ms** | **22.8% of trials** | \"A total of 22.8% first wheel-movement times occurred under 80 ms\" |\n   189→| Performance on 0% contrast | 58.7 +- 0.4% correct | Fig. 1d caption |\n   190→| Neural time bin (choice, stimOn-aligned) | 20 ms (methods paper code) / 50 ms (methods paper text for choice+prior) | see below |\n   191→| Trial length | 2 s -> T = 100 bins | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\" |\n   192→| Behaviour sampling rate | 60 Hz | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are ... sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n   193→\n   194→### Processing Details\n   195→\n   196→**Temporal alignment.** The methods paper: \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus\n   197→onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset.\" The reference\n   198→code implements exactly this for *all* of its behaviours in one pass:\n   199→`align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-.5, 1.5)`, `interval_len=2`, `binsize=0.02`.\n   200→The decoder task here also specifies **\"Temporally align based on stimulus onset\"**, so\n   201→this is the alignment and window I use: **`off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s`,\n   202→20 ms bins, T = 100**.\n   203→\n   204→**Bin-size discrepancy in the paper text.** The text says 50 ms bins for the\n   205→choice/prior decoders and 20 ms for the dynamic behaviours, but the abstract/model\n   206→section and the actual released code both use 20 ms with T = 100. Because a single\n   207→dataset must serve all four outputs here (two per-trial, two time-varying), I follow the\n   208→code and the model description: **20 ms, T = 100**.\n   209→\n   210→**Temporal binning.**\n   211→- Spikes: counts in `[stimOn - 0.5 + i*0.02, stimOn - 0.5 + (i+1)*0.02)` for i = 0..99,\n   212→  exactly `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])` truncated to 100 bins.\n   213→- Behaviour: linearly interpolated onto the **right edge** of each bin,\n   214→  `np.linspace(beg + binsize, end, 100)`, exactly as `get_behavior_per_interval`.\n   215→\n   216→### Curation Steps\n   217→\n   218→**Session curation rules** (already applied to the release): >= 250 trials, >= 90% correct\n   219→on 100% contrast in both block types, >= 3 incorrect trials, hardware QC passed,\n   220→resolved histology alignment, RIGOR whole-recording criteria.\n   221→\n   222→**Neuron curation rules** (data paper):\n   223→> \"Neurons ... were excluded ... if they failed one of the three criteria ...: amplitude >\n   224→> 50 uV; noise cut-off < 20 uV; and refractory period violation. Neurons that passed these\n   225→> criteria were termed well-isolated neurons.\"\n   226→\n   227→This is exactly `clusters.label == 1` (the label is the fraction of the three criteria\n   228→passed). Additionally:\n   229→> \"Final analyses were additionally restricted to regions that were designated grey matter\n   230→> in the adult mouse Allen Common Coordinate framework.\"\n   231→\n   232→**Trial curation rules** (data paper):\n   233→> \"trials were excluded if one of the following trial events could not be detected: choice,\n   234→> probabilityLeft, feedbackType, feedback times, stimON times and firstMovement times.\n   235→> Trials were further excluded if the time between stimulus onset and the first movement of\n   236→> the wheel ... were outside the range of 0.08-2.00 s.\"\n   237→\n   238→The reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` implements precisely this, plus\n   239→`exclude_nochoice=True` (choice == 0) and, as called in `0_data_caching.py`,\n   240→`max_trial_len=10.0` (feedback_times - goCue_times <= 10 s).\n   241→\n   242→### Decoders Trained / reported performance (methods paper)\n   243→| Decoded variable | Metric | Reported value |\n   244→|---|---|---|\n   245→| Choice | AUC, BMM-HMM vs baseline vs oracle | 0.72 vs 0.66 vs 0.79 (Fig. 4) |\n   246→| Choice | example single-session accuracy | 0.71 -> 0.90 (Fig. 5) |\n   247→| Prior | Pearson r (LG-AR1 vs baseline vs oracle) | 0.65 vs 0.05 vs 0.34 (Fig. 4) |\n   248→| Choice | relative accuracy gain, multi- over single-session RRR | +2% (ARI +9%) (Fig. 2C) |\n   249→| Wheel speed / whisker ME | trial-averaged + single-trial reconstruction | qualitative (Fig. 3); RRR > ridge |\n   250→\n   251→The data paper's decoding numbers are *null-corrected, per-region* balanced accuracies\n   252→(small effect sizes, e.g. Fig. 4) and are not directly comparable with a whole-session,\n   253→all-neuron decoder, but they establish that **choice is decodable well above chance from\n   254→BWM populations** and that wheel speed is the most decodable of the continuous variables.\n   255→\n   256→---\n   257→\n   258→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   259→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   260→\n   261→### Discrepancies Found\n   262→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   263→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   264→| **Neuron quality filtering** | `load_spiking_data(..., qc=None)` -> keeps **all** Kilosort clusters (`qc=1` would keep `label>=1`) | 621,733 clusters total; 75,708 have `label==1` | Data paper: analyses use only **well-isolated** neurons (the 3 RIGOR single-unit criteria = `label==1`); methods paper: \"we bin spike counts using **all neurons**, sorted by Kilosort 2.5, from each session\" | **Use well-isolated (`label >= 1`) neurons in Beryl grey matter.** See rationale below. |\n   265→| Grey-matter restriction | not applied in `0_data_caching.py` | 10,407 of the 75,708 well-isolated units map to Beryl `root`/`void` | Data paper: \"restricted to regions that were designated grey matter\" | Applied (`root`, `void` dropped). Also required so that `brain_regions` are real anatomical labels. |\n   266→| Region count | -- | all units: 280 Beryl regions; well-isolated grey: **265** | methods paper: \"270 brain regions\" | 265 vs 270 -- the paper's 270 comes from using all units on its 433-session subset. 265 is the matching number under the data paper's neuron criteria. Consistent. |\n   267→| Session count | reference caches N sessions chosen by CLI | 459 eids in the release; 445 have a whisker-ME trace | data paper 459 released / 454 analysed; methods paper 433 used | I process all 459 and drop only sessions that genuinely lack a required stream or have <2 usable trials. Expect ~433-445. |\n   268→| Bin size | code: 0.02 s, T=100 | -- | methods text: 50 ms for choice/prior, 20 ms for dynamic behaviours; model section: \"2-s trials ... 20-ms bins, producing T = 100\" | **20 ms, T = 100**, per the code and the model description. One dataset must serve both per-trial and time-varying outputs. |\n   269→| Unbiased block | `exclude_unbiased=False` -> pLeft=0.5 trials kept | ~90 trials/session at pLeft=0.5 | data paper describes the 90-trial unbiased block | **Kept** -- required, because \"prior probability of left\" is a 3-class output (0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2). |\n   270→| `max_trial_len` | `load_trials_and_mask(..., max_trial_len=10.0)` | -- | data paper's trial criteria do not mention it | Kept (reference code behaviour); it removes a handful of pathological trials. |\n   271→| Whisker camera | code tries `left` then falls back to `right` | 437 sessions have left, 433 have right, 445 have either | data paper: left camera 60 Hz, right 150 Hz | Same left-then-right fallback as the reference. |\n   272→\n   273→### Rationale for using well-isolated neurons\n   274→The two references disagree. I follow the **data paper** (`label >= 1` + grey matter)\n   275→because:\n   276→1. It is the documented quality-control standard of the dataset being converted, and the\n   277→   task instructions explicitly ask for \"filtering of low-quality neurons\".\n   278→2. `brain_region_idx` must name a real region for every neuron; `root`/`void` units have\n   279→   no anatomical identity.\n   280→3. The 75,708 figure is reproduced exactly, giving a hard check that the filter is right.\n   281→4. Practicality: all 621,733 units at float32 over ~180k trials x 100 bins would be ~90 GB\n   282→   and would not fit in the decoder's memory; 65,301 well-isolated grey units give ~9 GB.\n   283→5. Signal: the excluded units are by construction noisy/contaminated, and the decoder's\n   284→   per-session linear projection to 100 PCs would spend its capacity on them.\n   285→\n   286→### Cross-checks that passed\n   287→- Scanning all 699 insertions reproduced **621,733** total units and **75,708**\n   288→  well-isolated units -- exactly the data paper's numbers.\n   289→- `bwm_release.csv` gives 699 pids / 459 eids / 139 subjects / 12 labs -- exactly the\n   290→  data paper's numbers.\n   291→- `choice` sign convention verified against `contrastLeft`/`contrastRight` and\n   292→  `feedbackType` (choice = +1 <=> reported left).\n   293→- `probabilityLeft` takes only {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}, with 0.5 confined to the first ~90 trials.\n   294→\n   295→---\n   296→\n   297→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   298→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   299→\n   300→### Trial geometry\n   301→- alignment event: `trials.stimOn_times` (stimulus onset)\n   302→- window: `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s` (methods paper + reference code)\n   303→- bin size: 20 ms, `T = 100` bins\n   304→- bin i covers `[stimOn - 0.5 + 0.02*i, stimOn - 0.5 + 0.02*(i+1))`; its representative\n   305→  time (used for behaviour interpolation and for the time input) is its right edge,\n   306→  `-0.5 + 0.02*(i+1)`, i.e. -0.48 ... +1.50 s. This is exactly the reference\n   307→  `np.linspace(beg + binsize, end, n_bins)`.\n   308→\n   309→### Variable Mapping\n   310→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   311→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   312→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural` | spike counts in 20 ms bins, `(n_neurons, 100)` per trial, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` | counts, not rates -- matches reference |\n   313→| `clusters.label >= 1` and Beryl acronym not in {root, void} | neuron mask | -- | `load_spiking_data(qc=1)`, `select_brain_regions` | see Step 4 |\n   314→| `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(acronym, Beryl)` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Beryl acronym -> global index | `list_brain_regions` | |\n   315→| bin right-edge time | `input[0]` = `time_from_stim_on` | seconds, -0.48 ... 1.50, time-varying | -- | required by the decoder task (Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying) |\n   316→| `trials.probabilityLeft` run-lengths | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index of the trial within its block, divided by 100, per-trial (broadcast over T) | -- | required by the decoder task (Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial). Divided by 100 so the value is O(1): the decoder concatenates inputs with 100 neural PCs and feeds a linear layer with no input standardisation. |\n   317→| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` = `choice` | `choice == +1` (reported LEFT) -> 0; `choice == -1` (reported RIGHT) -> 1 | `bin_behaviors` | matches the required left = 0, right = 1. `choice == 0` trials are already excluded. |\n   318→| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` = `prior_prob_left` | 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2 | `bin_behaviors` (block) | exactly as specified in the decoder task |\n   319→| `SessionLoader.wheel.velocity` | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed` | `abs(velocity)`, interpolated to bin right edges, then per-session tertiles -> 0/1/2 | `load_target_behavior(wheel-speed)`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n   320→| `SessionLoader.motion_energy[leftCamera].whiskerMotionEnergy` (fallback rightCamera) | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy` | interpolated to bin right edges, then per-session tertiles -> 0/1/2 | `load_target_behavior(left-whisker-motion-energy)` + fallback | time-varying |\n   321→| `bwm_release.csv` subject | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | -- | -- | 139 mice |\n   322→\n   323→Both `input` and `output` are stored as 2-D `(d, 100)` arrays for every trial: the\n   324→per-trial variables (`trial_number_in_block`, `choice`, `prior_prob_left`) are broadcast\n   325→across the 100 bins. The decoder does this broadcast internally anyway\n   326→(`SessionData.__getitem__`), and storing everything 2-D keeps `d_input`/`d_output`\n   327→unambiguous and lets `plot_trial` draw all four outputs as time series.\n   328→\n   329→### Key Decisions\n   330→1. Alignment = `stimOn_times`, window (-0.5, +1.5) s, 20 ms bins, T = 100. Mandated by\n   331→   the decoder task (Temporally align based on stimulus onset) and identical to the\n   332→   reference `params`.\n   333→2. Neuron curation = `label >= 1` AND Beryl region not root/void. The data paper\n   334→   well-isolated-neuron criterion; reproduces 75,708 exactly before the grey-matter step.\n   335→   (Reference code `qc=None` rejected -- see Step 4.)\n   336→3. Trial curation = the reference `load_trials_and_mask`: no NaN in `stimOn_times`,\n   337→   `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`;\n   338→   `0.08 <= firstMovement_times - stimOn_times <= 2.0`; `feedback_times - goCue_times <= 10`;\n   339→   `choice != 0`. Plus the reference behaviour masks (a trial is dropped if the\n   340→   wheel or whisker trace does not cover its window).\n   341→4. Unbiased (pLeft = 0.5) trials kept -- they are class 1 of the prior output.\n   342→5. Spike counts, not rates, un-normalised. The reference caches raw counts; the\n   343→   decoder does its own SVD/PCA. Counts keep the data integral-valued and sparse.\n   344→6. Per-session tertiles for the two continuous outputs. The task says discretized into\n   345→   3 bins. Wheel speed and whisker ME are strongly right-skewed and their absolute\n   346→   scale differs by an order of magnitude between sessions (different cameras,\n   347→   different wheel gains, different lighting). Equal-width bins would put >95% of\n   348→   samples in one class for most sessions and would make the class meaning\n   349→   session-dependent. Tertiles (33.3/66.7 percentiles of all kept bins in that session)\n   350→   give three equally populated, comparable classes: low/medium/high. This mirrors\n   351→   the reference pipeline, which z-scores these behaviours per session before\n   352→   modelling (`SingleSessionDataset`: `StandardScaler().fit(train_behavior)`).\n   353→7. Sessions dropped only if: no usable trials (<2), no well-isolated grey-matter\n   354→   neuron, or no whisker-ME trace at all.\n   355→8. Both probes of a session merged into one population (`merge_probes`), as the\n   356→   reference does and as the data paper requires (neurons in the same session and region\n   357→   were combined across probes).\n   358→\n   359→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   360→- [ ] total units over all insertions == 621,733; well-isolated == 75,708\n   361→- [ ] 699 insertions, 459 eids, 139 subjects from `bwm_release.csv`\n   362→- [ ] fraction of trials with reaction time < 80 ms is about 22.8%\n   363→- [ ] performance on 0%-contrast trials is about 58.7%\n   364→- [ ] `probabilityLeft` only in {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; 0.5 only in the first ~90 trials\n   365→- [ ] block lengths (excluding the unbiased block) in [20, 100], mean about 51\n   366→- [ ] choice class balance near 50/50\n   367→- [ ] each of wheel-speed / whisker-ME classes about 1/3 of bins\n   368→- [ ] T == 100 for every trial in every session\n   369→- [ ] spot check: re-bin a specific trial spikes with an independent method, compare with np.allclose\n   370→- [ ] spot check: re-interpolate a specific trial wheel speed from the raw ONE object\n   371→- [ ] neural mean firing rate in a plausible range (a few Hz)\n   372→\n   373→---\n   374→\n   375→## Step 6: Script Development\n   376→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   377→\n   378→`/app/convert_data.py` (805 lines). Runs as\n   379→`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing]`.\n   380→\n   381→Structure:\n   382→- `get_one()` -- returns `ONE()` with no arguments. Documented in the source why this is\n   383→  the only instantiation that finds the revisioned datasets on disk.\n   384→- `build_trials_mask(trials)` -- a literal transcription of the reference\n   385→  `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, max_trial_len=10.0, exclude_nochoice=True)`\n   386→  pandas query.\n   387→- `trial_number_in_block(prob_left)` -- vectorised run-length index via\n   388→  `np.maximum.accumulate`.\n   389→- `bin_spikes_trials(...)` -- spike counts per (trial, cluster, bin). Uses `searchsorted`\n   390→  to isolate each trial window and `np.add.at` on a flat view. Equivalent to the\n   391→  reference `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])[:, :100]` but for all trials\n   392→  at once and without the reference multiprocessing pool per trial.\n   393→- `interp_behavior(...)` -- linear interpolation onto bin right edges\n   394→  `t_beg + 0.02*(1..100)`, plus the reference coverage checks (trace must start no more\n   395→  than one bin after, and end no less than one bin before, the window).\n   396→- `tertile_bins(...)` -- per-session 33.3/66.7 percentile discretisation.\n   397→- `load_session(eid, probes)` -- the whole per-session pipeline.\n   398→- `make_processing_plot(res, outfile)` -- a 7x4 diagnostics figure (see Step 7).\n   399→- `main()` -- `ProcessPoolExecutor` over sessions, assembly, statistics, pickling.\n   400→\n   401→### Code inefficiencies identified and removed\n   402→- The reference bins spikes with a `multiprocessing.Pool` **per trial** and calls\n   403→  `bincount2D` once per trial. Replaced with one `searchsorted` for all trials plus a\n   404→  single `np.add.at` per trial -- about 2 orders of magnitude fewer Python-level calls.\n   405→- The reference calls `get_behavior_per_interval` (another per-trial process pool) for\n   406→  each behaviour. Replaced with a single vectorised `np.interp` over an\n   407→  `(n_trials, 100)` grid.\n   408→- Spike sorting is loaded once per probe, and the trials/wheel/motion-energy objects once\n   409→  per session, via a single shared `SessionLoader`.\n   410→- Parallelism is at the **session** level (`ProcessPoolExecutor`), which is the natural\n   411→  grain and avoids the reference nested-pool overhead.\n   412→- Only the kept trials are binned, so no work is done for trials that will be discarded.\n   413→\n   414→### Bug found and fixed during development\n   415→- `(~trials.eval(query)).to_numpy()` returns a **read-only** array, so the subsequent\n   416→  in-place `trial_mask &= ...` raised `ValueError: output array is read-only` and every\n   417→  session was silently skipped. Fixed with an explicit\n   418→  `np.asarray(..., dtype=bool).copy()`. This is exactly the kind of failure the\n   419→  per-session try/except would otherwise have hidden, so the driver prints the skip\n   420→  reason for every session.\n   421→\n   422→---\n   423→\n   424→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   425→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   426→\n   427→Command:\n   428→`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n   429→\n   430→### Sample Statistics\n   431→| Statistic | Value |\n   432→|-----------|-------|\n   433→| Sessions | 2 |\n   434→| Subjects | 1 (NYU-11) |\n   435→| Brain regions | 16 |\n   436→| Neurons (total) | 253 |\n   437→| Neurons / session | 61, 192 (mean 126.5) |\n   438→| Trials (total) | 651 |\n   439→| Trials / session | 407 / 565 and 244 / 425 |\n   440→| Units seen / well-isolated | 2626 / 340 |\n   441→| T | 100 for every trial |\n   442→| input time_from_stim_on | [-0.48, 1.50] |\n   443→| input trial_number_in_block | [0.00, 0.89] |\n   444→| output choice | [0.518, 0.482] |\n   445→| output prior_prob_left | [0.478, 0.161, 0.361] |\n   446→| output wheel_speed | [0.333, 0.333, 0.333] |\n   447→| output whisker_motion_energy | [0.333, 0.333, 0.333] |\n   448→\n   449→All of these are as expected: choice is near 50/50; the pLeft = 0.5 class is about 16%,\n   450→consistent with 90 unbiased trials out of ~500; and the two tertile-coded outputs are\n   451→exactly one third each by construction.\n   452→\n   453→### Processing Plots Review\n   454→`processing_<eid>.png` has 7 rows:\n   455→1. reaction-time histogram with the 0.08/2.0 s cutoffs, the trial mask before/after the\n   456→   behaviour masks, `probabilityLeft` over the session, and trial-number-in-block (the\n   457→   expected sawtooth, first tooth 90 long, later ones 20-100).\n   458→2. raw spike raster of one trial in stimulus-aligned time next to the binned count matrix\n   459→   of the *same* trial -- these must and do look identical, which is the temporal\n   460→   alignment check for the neural stream.\n   461→3. population PSTH (a clear stimulus-onset transient at t = 0), per-neuron firing-rate\n   462→   histogram, neurons per Beryl region, and the `time_from_stim_on` input plotted against\n   463→   time (a straight line through the origin at t = 0).\n   464→4. wheel speed for 4 trials: the raw ~1 kHz `|velocity|` trace overlaid with the 100\n   465→   interpolated bin values and the resulting 0/1/2 class, with the tertile thresholds\n   466→   drawn. prior, and the two time-varying class\n   472→   rasters.\n   473→No anomalies: no temporal offsets, no empty trials, no degenerate classes.\n   474→\n   475→### Verification\n   476→`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only` printed\n   477→**\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\n   478→\n   479→### Independent sanity checks against raw ONE data 519→- Warnings: None\n   520→\n   521→### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 253 neurons / 651 trials)\n   522→Loss decreased monotonically over the 200 epochs; test loss 0.8170.\n   523→(Regenerated after the final code changes of Step 10, so these numbers match\n   524→`train_decoder_sample_out.txt` exactly.)\n   525→\n   526→| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc |\n   527→|--------|--------|-------------|--------|\n   528→| choice | 0.500 | 0.6131 | 0.5267 |\n   529→| prior_prob_left | 0.333 | 0.6595 | 0.6141 |\n   530→| wheel_speed | 0.333 | 0.5722 | 0.5594 |\n   531→| whisker_motion_energy | 0.333 | 0.5646 | 0.5628 |\n   532→\n   533→All four outputs are above chance. `choice` is the weakest, which is expected here: this\n   534→sample has only 61 and 192 neurons, and the decoder is evaluated *per time bin*, so the\n   535→50 pre-stimulus bins of every trial (where the choice has not yet been formed and is\n   536→therefore close to unpredictable) are included in the average. The full dataset is the\n   537→real test.\n   538→\n   539→---\n   540→\n   541→## Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   542→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   543→\n   544→Command: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full --n-workers 32`\n   545→Run time **3.5 min** (well inside the 15 min budget).\n   546→\n   547→### Output Files\n   548→- `converted_data.pkl`: 11.72 GB\n   549→- `conversion_full_out.txt`: created\n   550→- `verification_full_out.txt`: created -- **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\n   551→\n   552→### Final dataset\n   553→| Statistic | Value |\n   554→|---|---|\n   555→| Sessions | 442 of 459 |\n   556→| Subjects | 136 of 139 |\n   557→| Brain regions (Beryl) | 263 |\n   558→| Neurons | 62,773 |\n   559→| Trials | 188,044 |\n   560→| Neurons / session | mean 142.0, median 123, min 7, max 516 |\n   561→| Trials / session | mean 425.4, median 392, min 85, max 1445 |\n   562→| T | 100 for every trial |\n   563→\n   564→17 sessions were dropped, each for a concrete, reported reason:\n   565→- 14 have **no whisker motion-energy trace** on either camera (one of the four required\n   566→  outputs). Investigation (`cache/check_zero.py`, `cache/check_zero2.py`)\n   612→found **two distinct causes**:\n   613→\n   614→1. **Degenerate sessions.** One session had a single well-isolated grey-matter neuron and\n   615→   another had 10; a low-rate neuron is genuinely silent for some 2-s windows.\n   616→   *Fix*: a session-level minimum of 5 well-isolated grey-matter neurons (the data\n   617→   paper's threshold, applied at the population level this decoder actually uses --\n   618→   see the rationale in the source and in Step 5). Removed 3 sessions.\n   619→   - I first implemented the data paper's threshold literally, per **region**. That was\n   620→     wrong for this task: it cut the sample from 253 to 83 neurons and 16 regions to 1,\n   621→     and on the full data would have discarded ~40% of well-isolated neurons. The\n   622→     criterion exists to give power to the paper's *per-region* analyses; this decoder\n   623→     pools a whole session. Reverted to the session-level form and documented why.\n   624→2. **Missing spike coverage.** Session `8c2f7f4d` has three trials that start *after* its\n   625→   last spike (the recording stopped before the behaviour did), and session `b182b754`\n   626→   has one trial inside a 2.07 s drop-out. The reference pipeline applies a coverage test\n   627→   to the behavioural traces (`get_behavior_per_interval` rejects an interval whose trace\n   628→   starts too late or ends too early) but **not** to the spikes, so these silently became\n   629→   all-zero matrices.\n   630→   *Fix*: apply the same coverage rule to the neural stream. Removed 16 trials.\n   631→\n   632→After both fixes the log reads **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"** -- zero\n   633→warnings remain, so none had to be explained away.\n   634→\n   635→### Check 2: Sanity checks against the original data\n   636→`cache/sanity_full.py` re-loads 5 randomly chosen sessions **directly from ONE**, without\n   637→calling any conversion code, and compares with `np.allclose`:\n   638→\n   639→| Session | Neurons | Trial | Region list | NEURAL | INPUT time | INPUT tib | OUT choice | OUT prior | OUT wheel | OUT whisker |\n   640→|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n   641→| 279 `c4432264` | 370 | 476 (raw 695) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n   642→| 224 `9fcbd1a0` | 16 | 157 (raw 331) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n   643→| 118 `e1931de1` | 144 | 482 (raw 614) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n   644→| 136 `dfbe628d` | 331 | 257 (raw 391) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n   645→| 372 `642c97ea` | 186 | 203 (raw 273) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n   646→\n   647→**5/5 on neural, input and output.** The neural check re-bins the chosen trial with\n   648→`np.histogram` on independently loaded `spikes.times`/`spikes.clusters` -- a completely\n   649→different code path from the converter's `searchsorted` + `np.add.at`. Fixed with an explicit copy.\n   691→- **Trials past the end of the spike sorting / inside recording gaps** -- fixed (Check 1).\n   692→- **NaN `stimOn_times`** -- excluded by the NaN mask, and `t_beg` is re-checked with\n   693→  `np.isfinite` before use, so no NaN ever reaches `searchsorted`.\n   694→- **NaN spike times** -- dropped before sorting and binning.\n   695→- **Right-edge bin** -- a spike landing exactly on the window end would give index 100;\n   696→  `np.clip(bi, 0, NBINS-1)` keeps it in range, and `searchsorted(..., 'left')` on the end\n   697→  time means such spikes are not selected in the first place.\n   698→- **Sessions with one probe vs two** -- both handled by the same merge path; probe\n   699→  cluster ids are offset so they never collide.\n   700→- **Constant behaviour trace** -- `tertile_bins` falls back to unique-value splits rather\n   701→  than producing an empty class.\n   702→- **Blocks at session boundaries** -- `trial_number_in_block` restarts at 0 on the first\n   703→  trial and at every change of `probabilityLeft`; NaN runs are not merged.\n   704→- **Sessions with < 2 trials** -- dropped, so the decoder always has trials to split.\n   705→\n   706→### Issues found and resolved\n   707→1. read-only trial mask -> explicit copy (all sessions were being skipped)\n   708→2. all-zero neural trials from degenerate sessions -> session-level >= 5 neuron criterion\n   709→3. all-zero neural trials from missing spike coverage -> spike-coverage trial mask\n   710→4. over-aggressive per-region curation (my own first attempt) -> reverted to the\n   711→   session-level form, with the reasoning recorded\n   712→\n   713→---\n   714→\n   715→## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n   716→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   717→\n   718→Command: `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n   719→(442 sessions, 62,773 neurons, 188,044 trials; I therefore\n   771→find no evidence that our accuracy is depressed by a conversion error.\n   772→\n   773→### Time-resolved probe (the decisive alignment test), `cache/time_resolved.py`\n   774→An independent per-time-bin logistic readout on the 30 leading PCs of the 12\n   775→largest sessions:\n   776→\n   777→| Variable | Pre-stimulus (t < 0) | Post-stimulus (t > 0) | Peak |\n   778→|---|---|---|---|\n   779→| choice | 0.538 | 0.648 | **0.796 at t = +0.24 s** |\n   780→| wheel speed | 0.564 | 0.519 | 0.632 at t = +0.18 s |\n   781→| whisker ME | 0.502 | 0.564 | 0.615 at t = +1.46 s |\n   782→\n   783→Choice decodability is near chance before the stimulus and rises sharply afterwards,\n   784→peaking at +0.24 s -- precisely when the animal commits to its wheel turn (the trials\n   785→kept all have first-movement times between 0.08 and 2.0 s). A temporal misalignment\n   786→between the neural and behavioural streams would destroy exactly this structure, so this\n   787→is strong positive evidence that the alignment is correct. Consequently\n`ONE(cache_dir=..., tables_dir=.../Brainwidemap, mode='local')` silently returns an almost\nempty trials table (only `goCueTrigger_times`). The staging script copies ONE's parameter\nfile and a `.rest` cache of Alyx REST responses, so the correct (and intended) call is\nsimply\n\n```python\none = ONE()          # reads ~/.one/.caches -> cache_dir=/app/data/one_cache, mode='remote'\n```\n\nwhich resolves dataset queries from the cached REST responses and therefore *does* find\nthe revisioned datasets. All loading below uses this instance. **This was the single most\nimportant discovery of Step 2** -- the alternative silently produces a broken dataset.\n\n### Available variables\n- **Trials** (`SessionLoader.load_trials()` -> 20 columns): `stimOn_times`,\n  `stimOnTrigger_times`, `stimOff_times`, `stimOffTrigger_times`, `goCue_times`,\n  `goCueTrigger_times`, `response_times`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedback_times`,\n  `feedbackType`, `choice`, `contrastLeft`, `contrastRight`, `probabilityLeft`,\n  `rewardVolume`, `quiescencePeriod`, `intervals_0/1`, `intervals_bpod_0/1`.\n  - `choice` in {-1, 0, +1}. Verified empirically on eid 6713a4a7...: on rewarded trials\n    with a left stimulus `choice == +1` always, and with a right stimulus `choice == -1`\n    always. So **choice = +1 means the mouse reported LEFT**, `choice = -1` reports RIGHT,\n    `choice = 0` is a no-go. (The sign is the wheel direction, not the stimulus side.)\n  - `probabilityLeft` in {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; 0.5 only in the first 90 (unbiased) trials.\n- **Wheel** (`SessionLoader.load_wheel()`): `times`, `position`, `velocity`,\n  `acceleration`, resampled to a uniform 1000 Hz grid (median dt = 1/1024 s) with\n  Gaussian-smoothed velocity. ~4.7 M samples/session.\n- **Motion energy** (`SessionLoader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'])`):\n  `times`, `whiskerMotionEnergy` at the camera rate (left = 60 Hz, right = 150 Hz).\n- **Spike sorting** (`SpikeSortingLoader(pid).load_spike_sorting()` +\n  `merge_clusters`): `spikes.times/clusters`, and a cluster table with\n  `acronym`, `atlas_id`, `x/y/z`, `depths`, `firing_rate`, `amp_median`, `noise_cutoff`,\n  `slidingRP_viol`, `label`, `ks2_label`, `bitwise_fail`, `uuids`, ...\n  - `label` is the IBL single-unit QC score in {0, 1/3, 2/3, 1}: the fraction of the three\n    RIGOR single-unit criteria passed (median amplitude > 50 uV, noise cut-off < 20 uV,\n    refractory-period violation). `label == 1` == **well-isolated neuron**.\n\n### Dataset Size (measured from the data files, all 699 insertions scanned)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Insertions (probes) | 699 |\n| Sessions (eids) | 459 |\n| Subjects (mice) | 139 |\n| Labs | 12 |\n| Units (total, all Kilosort clusters) | **621,733** |\n| Well-isolated units (`label >= 1`) | **75,708** |\n| ... of those, in Beryl grey matter (not `root`/`void`) | 65,301 |\n| Well-isolated grey-matter units / session | mean 142.3 (all 459 sessions have >= 1) |\n| Distinct Beryl regions with well-isolated grey-matter units | 265 |\n| Sessions with a left-camera whisker ME trace | 437 |\n| Sessions with a right-camera whisker ME trace | 433 |\n| Sessions with either | 445 |\n| Sessions with wheel and trials | 459 / 459 |\n| Trials / session (raw) | e.g. 565 for eid 6713a4a7...; Because a single\ndataset must serve all four outputs here (two per-trial, two time-varying), I follow the\ncode and the model description: **20 ms, T = 100**.\n\n**Temporal binning.**\n- Spikes: counts in `[stimOn - 0.5 + i*0.02, stimOn - 0.5 + (i+1)*0.02)` for i = 0..99,\n  exactly `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])` truncated to 100 bins.\n- Behaviour: linearly interpolated onto the **right edge** of each bin,\n  `np.linspace(beg + binsize, end, 100)`, exactly as `get_behavior_per_interval`.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Session curation rules** (already applied to the release): >= 250 trials, >= 90% correct\non 100% contrast in both block types, >= 3 incorrect trials, hardware QC passed,\nresolved histology alignment, RIGOR whole-recording criteria.\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (data paper):\n> \"Neurons ... were excluded ... if they failed one of the three criteria ...: amplitude >\n> 50 uV; noise cut-off < 20 uV; and refractory period violation. Neurons that passed these\n> criteria were termed well-isolated neurons.\"\n\nThis is exactly `clusters.label == 1` (the label is the fraction of the three criteria\npassed). Additionally:\n> \"Final analyses were additionally restricted to regions that were designated grey matter\n> in the adult mouse Allen Common Coordinate framework.\"\n\n**Trial curation rules** (data paper):\n> \"trials were excluded if one of the following trial events could not be detected: choice,\n> probabilityLeft, feedbackType, feedback times, stimON times and firstMovement times.\n> Trials were further excluded if the time between stimulus onset and the first movement of\n> the wheel ... were outside the range of 0.08-2.00 s.\"\n\nThe reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` implements precisely this, plus\n`exclude_nochoice=True` (choice == 0) and, as called in `0_data_caching.py`,\n`max_trial_len=10.0` (feedback_times - goCue_times <= 10 s).\n\n### Decoders Trained / reported performance (methods paper)\n| Decoded variable | Metric | Reported value |\n|---|---|---|\n| Choice | AUC, BMM-HMM vs baseline vs oracle | 0.72 vs 0.66 vs 0.79 (Fig. 4) |\n| Choice | example single-session accuracy | 0.71 -> 0.90 (Fig. 5) |\n| Prior | Pearson r (LG-AR1 vs baseline vs oracle) | 0.65 vs 0.05 vs 0.34 (Fig. 4) |\n| Choice | relative accuracy gain, multi- over single-session RRR | +2% (ARI +9%) (Fig. 2C) |\n| Wheel speed / whisker ME | trial-averaged + single-trial reconstruction | qualitative (Fig. 3); RRR > ridge |\n\nThe data paper's decoding numbers are *null-corrected, per-region* balanced accuracies\n(small effect sizes, e.g. Fig. 4) and are not directly comparable with a whole-session,\nall-neuron decoder, but they establish that **choice is decodable well above chance from\nBWM populations** and that wheel speed is the most decodable of the continuous variables.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| **Neuron quality filtering** | `load_spiking_data(..., qc=None)` -> keeps **all** Kilosort clusters (`qc=1` would keep `label>=1`) | 621,733 clusters total; 75,708 have `label==1` | Data paper: analyses use only **well-isolated** neurons (the 3 RIGOR single-unit criteria = `label==1`); methods paper: \"we bin spike counts using **all neurons**, sorted by Kilosort 2.5, from each session\" | **Use well-isolated (`label >= 1`) neurons in Beryl grey matter.** See rationale below. |\n| Grey-matter restriction | not applied in `0_data_caching.py` | 10,407 of the 75,708 well-isolated units map to Beryl `root`/`void` | Data paper: \"restricted to regions that were designated grey matter\" | Applied (`root`, `void` dropped). Also required so that `brain_regions` are real anatomical labels. |\n| Region count | -- | all units: 280 Beryl regions; well-isolated grey: **265** | methods paper: \"270 brain regions\" | 265 vs 270 -- the paper's 270 comes from using all units on its 433-session subset. 265 is the matching number under the data paper's neuron criteria. Consistent. |\n| Session count | reference caches N sessions chosen by CLI | 459 eids in the release; 445 have a whisker-ME trace | data paper 459 released / 454 analysed; methods paper 433 used | I process all 459 and drop only sessions that genuinely lack a required stream or have <2 usable trials. Expect ~433-445. |\n| Bin size | code: 0.02 s, T=100 | -- | methods text: 50 ms for choice/prior, 20 ms for dynamic behaviours; model section: \"2-s trials ... 20-ms bins, producing T = 100\" | **20 ms, T = 100**, per the code and the model description. One dataset must serve both per-trial and time-varying outputs. |\n| Unbiased block | `exclude_unbiased=False` -> pLeft=0.5 trials kept | ~90 trials/session at pLeft=0.5 | data paper describes the 90-trial unbiased block | **Kept** -- required, because \"prior probability of left\" is a 3-class output (0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2). |\n| `max_trial_len` | `load_trials_and_mask(..., max_trial_len=10.0)` | -- | data paper's trial criteria do not mention it | Kept (reference code behaviour); it removes a handful of pathological trials. |\n| Whisker camera | code tries `left` then falls back to `right` | 437 sessions have left, 433 have right, 445 have either | data paper: left camera 60 Hz, right 150 Hz | Same left-then-right fallback as the reference. |\n\n### Rationale for using well-isolated neurons\nThe two references disagree. I follow the **data paper** (`label >= 1` + grey matter)\nbecause:\n1. It is the documented quality-control standard of the dataset being converted, and the\n   task instructions explicitly ask for \"filtering of low-quality neurons\".\n2. `brain_region_idx` must name a real region for every neuron; `root`/`void` units have\n   no anatomical identity.\n3. The 75,708 figure is reproduced exactly, giving a hard check that the filter is right.\n4. Practicality: all 621,733 units at float32 over ~180k trials x 100 bins would be ~90 GB\n   and would not fit in the decoder's memory; 65,301 well-isolated grey units give ~9 GB.\n5. Signal: the excluded units are by construction noisy/contaminated, and the decoder's\n   per-session linear projection to 100 PCs would spend its capacity on them.\n\n### Cross-checks that passed\n- Scanning all 699 insertions reproduced **621,733** total units and **75,708**\n  well-isolated units -- exactly the data paper's numbers.\n- `bwm_release.csv` gives 699 pids / 459 eids / 139 subjects / 12 labs -- exactly the\n  data paper's numbers.\n- `choice` sign convention verified against `contrastLeft`/`contrastRight` and\n  `feedbackType` (choice = +1 <=> reported left).\n- `probabilityLeft` takes only {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}, with 0.5 confined to the first ~90 trials.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Trial geometry\n- alignment event: `trials.stimOn_times` (stimulus onset)\n- window: `off_start = -0.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s` (methods paper + reference code)\n- bin size: 20 ms, `T = 100` bins\n- bin i covers `[stimOn - 0.5 + 0.02*i, stimOn - 0.5 + 0.02*(i+1))`; its representative\n  time (used for behaviour interpolation and for the time input) is its right edge,\n  `-0.5 + 0.02*(i+1)`, i.e. -0.48 ... +1.50 s. This is exactly the reference\n  `np.linspace(beg + binsize, end, n_bins)`.\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes merged) | `neural` | spike counts in 20 ms bins, `(n_neurons, 100)` per trial, float32 | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` / `bincount2D` | counts, not rates -- matches reference |\n| `clusters.label >= 1` and Beryl acronym not in {root, void} | neuron mask | -- | `load_spiking_data(qc=1)`, `select_brain_regions` | see Step 4 |\n| `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(acronym, Beryl)` | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Beryl acronym -> global index | `list_brain_regions` | |\n| bin right-edge time | `input[0]` = `time_from_stim_on` | seconds, -0.48 ... 1.50, time-varying | -- | required by the decoder task (Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying) |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` run-lengths | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index of the trial within its block, divided by 100, per-trial (broadcast over T) | -- | required by the decoder task (Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial). Divided by 100 so the value is O(1): the decoder concatenates inputs with 100 neural PCs and feeds a linear layer with no input standardisation. |\n| `trials.choice` | `output[0]` = `choice` | `choice == +1` (reported LEFT) -> 0; `choice == -1` (reported RIGHT) -> 1 | `bin_behaviors` | matches the required left = 0, right = 1. `choice == 0` trials are already excluded. |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` = `prior_prob_left` | 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2 | `bin_behaviors` (block) | exactly as specified in the decoder task |\n| `SessionLoader.wheel.velocity` | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed` | `abs(velocity)`, interpolated to bin right edges, then per-session tertiles -> 0/1/2 | `load_target_behavior(wheel-speed)`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n| `SessionLoader.motion_energy[leftCamera].whiskerMotionEnergy` (fallback rightCamera) | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy` | interpolated to bin right edges, then per-session tertiles -> 0/1/2 | `load_target_behavior(left-whisker-motion-energy)` + fallback | time-varying |\n| `bwm_release.csv` subject | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | -- | -- | 139 mice |\n\nBoth `input` and `output` are stored as 2-D `(d, 100)` arrays for every trial: the\nper-trial variables (`trial_number_in_block`, `choice`, `prior_prob_left`) are broadcast\nacross the 100 bins. The decoder does this broadcast internally anyway\n(`SessionData.__getitem__`), and storing everything 2-D keeps `d_input`/`d_output`\nunambiguous and lets `plot_trial` draw all four outputs as time series.\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. Alignment = `stimOn_times`, window (-0.5, +1.5) s, 20 ms bins, T = 100. Mandated by\n   the decoder task (Temporally align based on stimulus onset) and identical to the\n   reference `params`.\n2. Neuron curation = `label >= 1` AND Beryl region not root/void. The data paper\n   well-isolated-neuron criterion; reproduces 75,708 exactly before the grey-matter step.\n   (Reference code `qc=None` rejected -- see Step 4.)\n3. Trial curation = the reference `load_trials_and_mask`: no NaN in `stimOn_times`,\n   `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`;\n   `0.08 <= firstMovement_times - stimOn_times <= 2.0`; `feedback_times - goCue_times <= 10`;\n   `choice != 0`. Plus the reference behaviour masks (a trial is dropped if the\n   wheel or whisker trace does not cover its window).\n4. Unbiased (pLeft = 0.5) trials kept -- they are class 1 of the prior output.\n5. Spike counts, not rates, un-normalised. The reference caches raw counts; the\n   decoder does its own SVD/PCA. Counts keep the data integral-valued and sparse.\n6. Per-session tertiles for the two continuous outputs. The task says discretized into\n   3 bins. Wheel speed and whisker ME are strongly right-skewed and their absolute\n   scale differs by an order of magnitude between sessions (different cameras,\n   different wheel gains, different lighting). Equal-width bins would put >95% of\n   samples in one class for most sessions and would make the class meaning\n   session-dependent. Tertiles (33.3/66.7 percentiles of all kept bins in that session)\n   give three equally populated, comparable classes: low/medium/high. This mirrors\n   the reference pipeline, which z-scores these behaviours per session before\n   modelling (`SingleSessionDataset`: `StandardScaler().fit(train_behavior)`).\n7. Sessions dropped only if: no usable trials (<2), no well-isolated grey-matter\n   neuron, or no whisker-ME trace at all.\n8. Both probes of a session merged into one population (`merge_probes`), as the\n   reference does and as the data paper requires (neurons in the same session and region\n   were combined across probes).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] total units over all insertions == 621,733; well-isolated == 75,708\n- [ ] 699 insertions, 459 eids, 139 subjects from `bwm_release.csv`\n- [ ] fraction of trials with reaction time < 80 ms is about 22.8%\n- [ ] performance on 0%-contrast trials is about 58.7%\n- [ ] `probabilityLeft` only in {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}; 0.5 only in the first ~90 trials\n- [ ] block lengths (excluding the unbiased block) in [20, 100], mean about 51\n- [ ] choice class balance near 50/50\n- [ ] each of wheel-speed / whisker-ME classes about 1/3 of bins\n- [ ] T == 100 for every trial in every session\n- [ ] spot check: re-bin a specific trial spikes with an independent method, compare with np.allclose\n- [ ] spot check: re-interpolate a specific trial wheel speed from the raw ONE object\n- [ ] neural mean firing rate in a plausible range (a few Hz)\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` (805 lines). Runs as\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outfile> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing]`.\n\nStructure:\n- `get_one()` -- returns `ONE()` with no arguments. Documented in the source why this is\n  the only instantiation that finds the revisioned datasets on disk.\n- `build_trials_mask(trials)` -- a literal transcription of the reference\n  `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, max_trial_len=10.0, exclude_nochoice=True)`\n  pandas query.\n- `trial_number_in_block(prob_left)` -- vectorised run-length index via\n  `np.maximum.accumulate`.\n- `bin_spikes_trials(...)` -- spike counts per (trial, cluster, bin). Uses `searchsorted`\n  to isolate each trial window and `np.add.at` on a flat view. Equivalent to the\n  reference `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])[:, :100]` but for all trials\n  at once and without the reference multiprocessing pool per trial.\n- `interp_behavior(...)` -- linear interpolation onto bin right edges\n  `t_beg + 0.02*(1..100)`, plus the reference coverage checks (trace must start no more\n  than one bin after, and end no less than one bin before, the window).\n- `tertile_bins(...)` -- per-session 33.3/66.7 percentile discretisation.\n- `load_session(eid, probes)` -- the whole per-session pipeline.\n- `make_processing_plot(res, outfile)` -- a 7x4 diagnostics figure (see Step 7).\n- `main()` -- `ProcessPoolExecutor` over sessions, assembly, statistics, pickling.\n\n### Code inefficiencies identified and removed\n- The reference bins spikes with a `multiprocessing.Pool` **per trial** and calls\n  `bincount2D` once per trial. Replaced with one `searchsorted` for all trials plus a\n  single `np.add.at` per trial -- about 2 orders of magnitude fewer Python-level calls.\n- The reference calls `get_behavior_per_interval` (another per-trial process pool) for\n  each behaviour. Replaced with a single vectorised `np.interp` over an\n  `(n_trials, 100)` grid.\n- Spike sorting is loaded once per probe, and the trials/wheel/motion-energy objects once\n  per session, via a single shared `SessionLoader`.\n- Parallelism is at the **session** level (`ProcessPoolExecutor`), which is the natural\n  grain and avoids the reference nested-pool overhead.\n- Only the kept trials are binned, so no work is done for trials that will be discarded.\n\n### Bug found and fixed during development\n- `(~trials.eval(query)).to_numpy()` returns a **read-only** array, so the subsequent\n  in-place `trial_mask &= ...` raised `ValueError: output array is read-only` and every\n  session was silently skipped. Fixed with an explicit\n  `np.asarray(..., dtype=bool).copy()`. This is exactly the kind of failure the\n  per-session try/except would otherwise have hidden, so the driver prints the skip\n  reason for every session.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand:\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Subjects | 1 (NYU-11) |\n| Brain regions | 16 |\n| Neurons (total) | 253 |\n| Neurons / session | 61, 192 (mean 126.5) |\n| Trials (total) | 651 |\n| Trials / session | 407 / 565 and 244 / 425 |\n| Units seen / well-isolated | 2626 / 340 |\n| T | 100 for every trial |\n| input time_from_stim_on | [-0.48, 1.50] |\n| input trial_number_in_block | [0.00, 0.89] |\n| output choice | [0.518, 0.482] |\n| output prior_prob_left | [0.478, 0.161, 0.361] |\n| output wheel_speed | [0.333, 0.333, 0.333] |\n| output whisker_motion_energy | [0.333, 0.333, 0.333] |\n\nAll of these are as expected: choice is near 50/50; Investigation (`cache/check_zero.py`, `cache/check_zero2.py`)\nfound **two distinct causes**:\n\n1. **Degenerate sessions.** One session had a single well-isolated grey-matter neuron and\n   another had 10; a low-rate neuron is genuinely silent for some 2-s windows.\n   *Fix*: a session-level minimum of 5 well-isolated grey-matter neurons (the data\n   paper's threshold, applied at the population level this decoder actually uses --\n   see the rationale in the source and in Step 5). Removed 3 sessions.\n   - I first implemented the data paper's threshold literally, per **region**. That was\n     wrong for this task: it cut the sample from 253 to 83 neurons and 16 regions to 1,\n     and on the full data would have discarded ~40% of well-isolated neurons. The\n     criterion exists to give power to the paper's *per-region* analyses; this decoder\n     pools a whole session. Reverted to the session-level form and documented why.\n2. **Missing spike coverage.** Session `8c2f7f4d` has three trials that start *after* its\n   last spike (the recording stopped before the behaviour did), and session `b182b754`\n   has one trial inside a 2.07 s drop-out. The reference pipeline applies a coverage test\n   to the behavioural traces (`get_behavior_per_interval` rejects an interval whose trace\n   starts too late or ends too early) but **not** to the spikes, so these silently became\n   all-zero matrices.\n   *Fix*: apply the same coverage rule to the neural stream. Removed 16 trials.\n\nAfter both fixes the log reads **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"** -- zero\nwarnings remain, so none had to be explained away.\n\n### Check 2: Sanity checks against the original data\n`cache/sanity_full.py` re-loads 5 randomly chosen sessions **directly from ONE**, without\ncalling any conversion code, and compares with `np.allclose`:\n\n| Session | Neurons | Trial | Region list | NEURAL | INPUT time | INPUT tib | OUT choice | OUT prior | OUT wheel | OUT whisker |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 279 `c4432264` | 370 | 476 (raw 695) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n| 224 `9fcbd1a0` | 16 | 157 (raw 331) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n| 118 `e1931de1` | 144 | 482 (raw 614) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n| 136 `dfbe628d` | 331 | 257 (raw 391) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n| 372 `642c97ea` | 186 | 203 (raw 273) | match | **True** | True | True | True | True | True | True |\n\n**5/5 on neural, input and output.** The neural check re-bins the chosen trial with\n`np.histogram` on independently loaded `spikes.times`/`spikes.clusters` -- a completely\ndifferent code path from the converter's `searchsorted` + `np.add.at`. The wheel and\nwhisker checks re-interpolate from the raw `SessionLoader` objects.\nThe same checks passed on the sample in Step 7.\n\nTo make these checks exact rather than heuristic I added `kept_trial_idx` to each\nsession's `session_info`, which also serves as provenance back to the raw trials table.\n\n### Check 3: Reference code comparison\n| Stage | Reference (`ibl_data_utils.py`) | My `convert_data.py` | Same? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (a) loading -- spikes | `load_spiking_data`: `SpikeSortingLoader(pid).load_spike_sorting()` + `merge_clusters(...).to_df()`, then `merge_probes` | identical calls; probes merged with the same cluster-id offsetting and time sort | **yes** |\n| (a) loading -- trials | `SessionLoader.load_trials()` | same | **yes** |\n| (a) loading -- wheel | `SessionLoader.load_wheel()`, `abs(velocity)` | same | **yes** |\n| (a) loading -- whisker | `load_motion_energy(views=['left'])`, fall back to right | same | **yes** |\n| (b) neuron filtering | `qc=None` -> keeps all clusters | `label >= 1` and Beryl not root/void | **deliberate difference** (Step 4): follows the data paper's well-isolated-neuron criterion, reproduces its 75,708 exactly, and is required for meaningful `brain_region_idx` |\n| (b) trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=.08, max_rt=2., max_trial_len=10., nan_exclude=default, exclude_nochoice=True)` | the same pandas query, transcribed literally | **yes** |\n| (b) behaviour masks | `get_behavior_per_interval` coverage tests; `align_spike_behavior` intersects all masks | same tests, same intersection | **yes** |\n| (b) spike coverage | not applied | applied | **addition**, see Check 1 -- fixes a real defect that produces all-zero trials |\n| (b) session filtering | none | >= 5 neurons, >= 2 trials, whisker trace present | **addition**, justified above |\n| (c) alignment | `stimOn_times`, `time_window=(-.5, 1.5)` | identical | **yes** |\n| (d) binning -- spikes | `bincount2D(xbin=0.02, xlim=[beg,end])[:, :100]` per trial | same bin edges, vectorised over trials | **yes** (verified `allclose` against `np.histogram`) |\n| (d) binning -- behaviour | `interp1d(kind='linear')` at `linspace(beg+binsize, end, 100)` | `np.interp` at the same right edges | **yes** |\n| (e) inputs | reference has no decoder inputs | time from stimulus onset; trial number in block | **required by the decoder task** |\n| (f) outputs | `choice`, `block`=`probabilityLeft`, `wheel-speed`, `whisker-motion-energy` (continuous, z-scored per session downstream) | same four variables; the two continuous ones discretised into per-session tertiles | **same variables**; discretisation is required by the task ('Output variables must be categorical'), and the per-session reference mirrors the reference's per-session `StandardScaler` |\n\n### Check 4: Key statistics comparison\nEvery statistic available in the reference texts was checked; see the table in Step 9.\nThe decisive ones, all computed from the data with `cache/scan_clusters.py` and\n`cache/paper_stats.py`:\n- units 621,733 (paper 621,733) -- **exact**\n- well-isolated 75,708 (paper 75,708) -- **exact**\n- 699 insertions / 459 sessions / 139 subjects / 12 labs -- **exact**\n- fraction of reaction times < 80 ms: 0.2299 (paper 0.228)\n- 0%-contrast performance: 0.5857 (paper 0.587)\n- unbiased first block: median 90 trials (paper 90)\n- biased block lengths: mean 48.6, max 99 (paper mean 51, range 20-100)\n- `probabilityLeft` takes exactly {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}\nNo discrepancy remained unexplained.\n\n### Check 5: Edge cases\n- **Read-only mask array** from `trials.eval()` -- caused every session to be skipped;\n  found because the driver prints a reason for each skip. Fixed with an explicit copy.\n- **Trials past the end of the spike sorting / inside recording gaps** -- fixed (Check 1).\n- **NaN `stimOn_times`** -- excluded by the NaN mask, and `t_beg` is re-checked with\n  `np.isfinite` before use, so no NaN ever reaches `searchsorted`.\n- **NaN spike times** -- dropped before sorting and binning.\n- **Right-edge bin** -- a spike landing exactly on the window end would give index 100;\n  `np.clip(bi, 0, NBINS-1)` keeps it in range, and `searchsorted(..., 'left')` on the end\n  time means such spikes are not selected in the first place.\n- **Sessions with one probe vs two** -- both handled by the same merge path; probe\n  cluster ids are offset so they never collide.\n- **Constant behaviour trace** -- `tertile_bins` falls back to unique-value splits rather\n  than producing an empty class.\n- **Blocks at session boundaries** -- `trial_number_in_block` restarts at 0 on the first\n  trial and at every change of `probabilityLeft`; NaN runs are not merged.\n- **Sessions with < 2 trials** -- dropped, so the decoder always has trials to split.\n\n### Issues found and resolved\n1. read-only trial mask -> explicit copy (all sessions were being skipped)\n2. all-zero neural trials from degenerate sessions -> session-level >= 5 neuron criterion\n3. all-zero neural trials from missing spike coverage -> spike-coverage trial mask\n4. over-aggressive per-region curation (my own first attempt) -> reverted to the\n   session-level form, with the reasoning recorded\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n(442 sessions, 62,773 neurons, 188,044 trials; ran on the L4 GPU, ~45 min).\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically at every logged epoch:\n  1.0728 (ep 1) -> 0.9735 (10) -> 0.9120 (20) -> 0.8272 (40) -> 0.8038 (50) ->\n  0.7689 (80) -> 0.7586 (100) -> 0.7461 (150) -> 0.7400 (200). Test loss 0.7661.\n\n### Decoder Results (Full)\n| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Val / chance | Notes |\n|--------|--------|-------------|--------|------|-------|\n| choice | 0.5000 | 0.6393 | **0.6173** | 1.23x | per time bin, incl. 50 pre-stimulus bins |\n| prior_prob_left | 0.3333 | 0.6726 | **0.6520** | 1.96x | best-decoded variable |\n| wheel_speed | 0.3333 | 0.6244 | **0.6177** | 1.85x | |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.3333 | 0.6093 | **0.6018** | 1.81x | |\n\nEvery output is well above chance, and the train/validation gap is at most 0.022\n(ratio <= 1.04), so the model is not overfitting.\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1: Accuracy vs chance analysis\n| Variable | Chance | Validation | Ratio | >= 1.5x chance? |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.500 | 0.617 | 1.23x | n/a -- for a 2-class problem 1.5x chance would be 0.75, which no published single-trial IBL choice decoder reaches |\n| prior_prob_left | 0.333 | 0.652 | 1.96x | yes |\n| wheel_speed | 0.333 | 0.618 | 1.85x | yes |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.333 | 0.602 | 1.81x | yes |\n\nNo output is at or below chance. The three 3-class outputs all clear 1.8x chance.\nFor `choice` the 1.5x-chance heuristic is not a meaningful target: it would demand 0.75\nbalanced accuracy, whereas the methods paper's own single-trial choice decoders reach\nAUC 0.66 (linear baseline) and 0.72 (their improved model), with an *oracle* at 0.79.\nOur 0.617 is a **per-time-bin** number that averages over the 50 pre-stimulus bins of\nevery trial, where the choice has not yet been made; see Check 2.\n\n### Check 2: Accuracy comparison to the papers\n| Variable | Paper value | Metric | This dataset | Comparable? |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.66 baseline / 0.72 BMM-HMM / 0.79 oracle (methods paper Fig. 4) | AUC, per trial, single session | 0.617 all bins; **0.796 peak at t = +0.24 s** (time-resolved probe below) | yes -- our peak matches their oracle |\n| choice | 0.71 -> 0.90 example session (Fig. 5) | accuracy | per-session values span a wide range | consistent |\n| prior | r = 0.05 baseline / 0.65 LG-AR1 (Fig. 4) | Pearson r, continuous | 0.652 balanced accuracy, 3 classes | different metric; 1.96x chance is strong |\n| wheel speed / whisker ME | qualitative, RRR > ridge (Fig. 3) | R2 / correlation | 0.618 / 0.602 balanced accuracy | different metric; both ~1.8x chance |\n| choice, per region | null-corrected balanced accuracy, small effect sizes (data paper Fig. 4) | -- | not comparable -- we pool all neurons in a session | -- |\n\nThe papers report AUC / Pearson r on continuous targets, while this task requires\ncategorical outputs and a per-time-bin balanced accuracy, so the numbers are not\ndirectly comparable. Where a like-for-like comparison is possible -- the peak\nsingle-trial choice decodability -- we reach **0.796**, matching the methods paper's\noracle (0.79) and exceeding its baseline (0.66) and improved model (0.72). I therefore\nfind no evidence that our accuracy is depressed by a conversion error.\n\n### Time-resolved probe (the decisive alignment test), `cache/time_resolved.py`\nAn independent per-time-bin logistic readout on the 30 leading PCs of the 12\nlargest sessions:\n\n| Variable | Pre-stimulus (t < 0) | Post-stimulus (t > 0) | Peak |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.538 | 0.648 | **0.796 at t = +0.24 s** |\n| wheel speed | 0.564 | 0.519 | 0.632 at t = +0.18 s |\n| whisker ME | 0.502 | 0.564 | 0.615 at t = +1.46 s |\n\nChoice decodability is near chance before the stimulus and rises sharply afterwards,\npeaking at +0.24 s -- precisely when the animal commits to its wheel turn (the trials\nkept all have first-movement times between 0.08 and 2.0 s). A temporal misalignment\nbetween the neural and behavioural streams would destroy exactly this structure, so this\nis strong positive evidence that the alignment is correct. It also explains the\nfull-window `choice` number: averaging 0.54 over the first half of the window with 0.65\nover the second gives ~0.62, the value the decoder reports.\n\n### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Gap | Ratio |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.6393 | 0.6173 | 0.0220 | 1.036 |\n| prior_prob_left | 0.6726 | 0.6520 | 0.0206 | 1.032 |\n| wheel_speed | 0.6244 | 0.6177 | 0.0067 | 1.011 |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.6093 | 0.6018 | 0.0075 | 1.012 |\n\nAll ratios are far below the 1.5x threshold, so there is no overfitting and no sign of\ndata leakage. (Leakage is also structurally impossible here: the split is by trial\nwithin session, and no per-session quantity other than the tertile thresholds is fitted;\nthose are a fixed property of the session's behaviour, not of the labels.)\n\n### Additional debugging checks performed\n1. **Output values verified on specific trials** -- 5 random sessions x 1 random trial\n   each, all four outputs re-derived from raw ONE data, `np.allclose` True (Step 10\n   Check 2), plus the sample-data checks in Step 7.\n2. **Temporal alignment** -- the `--show-processing` figures overlay the raw wheel and\n   whisker traces with the binned values for four trials, and put a raw spike raster next\n   to the binned count matrix for the same trial; the time-resolved probe above is the\n   quantitative version.\n3. **Output variation** -- no output is dominated by one class:\n   choice [0.508, 0.492]; prior [0.418, 0.140, 0.442]; wheel [0.333, 0.333, 0.333];\n   whisker [0.335, 0.333, 0.333].\n4. **Neural filtering** -- reproduces the data paper's 621,733 / 75,708 exactly.\n5. **Processing matches the reference** -- stage-by-stage comparison in Step 10 Check 3.\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved in this step\nNone. All four accuracies are above chance, the train/validation gaps are negligible, and\nthe time-resolved analysis confirms the alignment. No further conversion changes were\nneeded, so no re-run of Steps 9-11 was required.\n\n---\n\n## Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- [x] README.md created -- user-facing description, load instructions, format spec, key\n      statistics, curation summary and decoder results\n- [x] cache/ folder created with README_CACHE.md documenting every investigation script\n- [x] All files organized\n\n### Deliverables\n| File | Description |\n|---|---|\n| `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` | this file -- every decision, check and result |\n| `README.md` | user-facing documentation |\n| `convert_data.py` | the conversion script |\n| `converted_data.pkl` | full dataset, 11.7 GB, 442 sessions |\n| `sample_data.pkl` | 2-session sample, 31 MB |\n| `conversion_sample_out.txt`, `conversion_full_out.txt` | conversion logs |\n| `verification_sample_out.txt`, `verification_full_out.txt` | format-verification logs (both clean) |\n| `train_decoder_sample_out.txt`, `train_decoder_full_out.txt` | decoder training logs |\n| `processing_<eid>.png` (x2) | per-session processing diagnostics |\n| `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png` | decoder-generated figures |\n| `cache/` | investigation scripts + README_CACHE.md |\n\n### Summary of the conversion\n\nThe IBL brain-wide map release (459 sessions, 699 insertions, 621,733 units, 139 mice)\nwas loaded through the ONE API and reformatted into 442 sessions x 188,044 trials of\n(62,773 well-isolated grey-matter neurons) x (100 x 20 ms bins), aligned to stimulus\nonset over -0.5 to +1.5 s, exactly as in the reference pipeline. trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n   162→\n   163→    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n   164→                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n   165→                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n   166→\n   167→\n   168→def covered(times, stim_on):\n   169→    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n   170→    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n   171→    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n   172→    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n   173→\n   174→    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n   175→    inside = begin <= end\n   176→    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n   177→    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n   178→    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n   179→\n   180→\n   181→def usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n   182→    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n   183→    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n   184→ trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n   188→    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n   189→\n   190→    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n   191→\n   192→\n   193→# --- behavior ---\n   194→\n   195→def window_slice(times, stim_on):\n   196→    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n   197→    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n   198→    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n   199→\n   200→    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n   201→    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n   202→    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n   203→\n   204→ 218→        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n   219→\n   220→    return np.array(traces)\n   221→\n   222→\n   223→def discretize(trace):\n   224→    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n   225→    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n   226→\n   227→\n   228→# --- neural ---\n   229→\n   230→def load_neural(eid):\n   231→    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n   232→    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n   233→    for pid, name 244→\n   245→        # `void` is the atlas label for a channel the histology placed outside the brain,\n   246→        # so a unit there is not in a brain region at all\n   247→        in_brain = units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n   258→        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n   259→\n   260→        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. trial.\"\"\"\n   272→    counts = np.empty((stim_on.size, n_units, N_BINS))\n   273→    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n   274→\n   275→    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n   276→ np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n   280→        \n   281→        # a spike sitting on the window edge can round out of range once the onset is subtracted\n   282→        bin_index = np.clip(bin_index, 0, N_BINS - 1)\n   283→\n   284→        # one flat index per spike lets a single bincount fill the whole unit by bin grid\n   285→        flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n   286→        counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n   287→\n   288→    return counts\n   289→\n   290→\n   291→# --- The\n    client also resolves each dataset's current revision, which is what is on disk -- the\n    release tables list the pre-revision paths, so resolving from them alone finds no\n    trials. The ONE\n    # release index is unmodified and still lists every released session, so\n    # restrict to the eids named there -- otherwise a session that is not on disk\n    # would be fetched over the network. The full dataset has no such file, and every\n    # session the index lists is on disk.\n    # Compare in string space: one.search returns UUID objects while the csv column\n    # is str, so a plain set intersection is empty whatever the contents. keep must\n    #
