NaN compares false, so those go too\n    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\n    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n\n\n# --- behavior ---\n\ndef window_slice(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n\n    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n\n    return begin, end\n\n\ndef trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    traces = []\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n        last = end[trial] + 1\n\n        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n\n    return np.array(traces)\n\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_neural(eid):\n    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n\n        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n\n        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n        if not clusters:\n            continue\n\n        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n\n        # `void` is the atlas label for a channel the histology placed outside the brain,\n        # so a unit there is not in a brain region at all\n        in_brain = brain_regions.acronym2acronym(\n            clusters['acronym'], mapping='Beryl') != 'void'\n\n        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n        is_good = (clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL) & in_brain\n        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n\n        # renumber the surviving clusters, continuing the count from the previous probe\n        new_index = np.cumsum(is_good) - 1\n        units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n\n        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. DG-sg and DG-mo both become DG\n        regions.extend(brain_regions.acronym2acronym(clusters['acronym'][is_good], mapping='Beryl'))\n\n    times, units = np.concatenate(times), np.concatenate(units)\n\n    # sort the merged probes by spike time, as the reference does, so trials can be sliced\n    order = np.argsort(times, kind='stable')\n    return times[order], units[order], np.array(regions)\n\n\ndef spike_counts(times, units, n_units, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Spike count per unit and bin, for every trial.\"\"\"\n    counts = np.empty((stim_on.size, n_units, N_BINS))\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # time from stimulus onset\n        spike_unit = units[begin[trial]:end[trial]]\n\n        bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        \n        # a spike sitting on the window edge can round out of range once the onset is subtracted\n        bin_index = np.clip(bin_index, 0, N_BINS - 1)\n\n        # one flat index per spike lets a single bincount fill the whole unit by bin grid\n        flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n        counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n\n    return counts\n\n\n# --- session ---\n\ndef process_session(eid, subject):\n    \"\"\"One session as lists of per trial neural, input and output arrays.\"\"\"\n    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n    loader.load_trials()\n    loader.load_wheel()\n    view = camera_view(eid)\n    loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n\n    trials, wheel = loader.trials, loader.wheel\n    motion_energy = loader.motion_energy[f'{view}Camera']\n\n    variables = trial_variables(trials)                 # before filtering, so the block count is real\n    keep = usable_trials(trials, wheel.times.to_numpy(), motion_energy.times.to_numpy())\n    variables = variables[keep]\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()[keep]\n\n    # no trial survived the mask, so there is nothing to load or bin\n    if stim_on.size == 0:\n        return {'session_id': str(eid), 'subject': subject, 'camera': view,\n                'neural': [], 'input': [], 'output': [],\n                'n_trials': 0, 'n_units': 0, 'regions': np.array([])}\n\n    speed = trial_traces(wheel.times, np.abs(wheel.velocity), stim_on)\n    whisker = trial_traces(motion_energy.times, motion_energy.iloc[:, 1], stim_on)\n\n    spike_times, spike_units, regions = load_neural(eid)\n    rate = spike_counts(spike_times, spike_units, regions.size, stim_on) / BIN\n\n    # the split is over the whole session, so both traces are discretized once, not per trial\n    speed_class = discretize(speed)\n    whisker_class = discretize(whisker)\n\n    neural = [trial.astype(np.float32) for trial in rate]\n    inputs, outputs = [], []\n    for trial in range(stim_on.size):\n        row = variables.iloc[trial]\n        inputs.append(np.stack([TIME, np.full(N_BINS, row.trial_in_block)]).astype(np.float32))\n        outputs.append(np.stack([np.full(N_BINS, row.choice), np.full(N_BINS, row.prior),\n                                 speed_class[trial], whisker_class[trial]]).astype(np.int8))\n\n    return {'session_id': str(eid), 'subject': subject, 'camera': view,\n            'neural': neural, 'input': inputs, 'output': outputs,\n            'n_trials': len(neural), 'n_units': regions.size, 'regions': regions}\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    regions = sorted({region for r in results for region in r['regions']})\n    reg_ix = {region: i for i, region in enumerate(regions)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n\n        'brain_regions': regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_ix[x] for x in r['regions']], np.int32) for r in results],\n\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description':\n                'Head-fixed mice turn a wheel to report the side of a visual grating. The '\n                'probability that the stimulus appears on the left is held constant within a '\n                'block and switches between 0.2 and 0.8.',\n            'time_bin_size': BIN * 1000.0,          # ms\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'visual stimulus onset (stimOn_times)',\n            'off_start': T_START,\n            'off_end': T_STOP,\n\n            'dataset': 'IBL brain-wide map, release tag %s' % RELEASE,\n            'neural_units': 'Hz',\n            'session_info': [\n                {'session_id': r['session_id'], 'subject': r['subject'], 'camera': r['camera'],\n                 'n_trials': r['n_trials'], 'n_units': r['n_units']}\n                for r in results\n            ],\n        },\n    }\n\n\ndef worker_setup(datadir):\n    \"\"\"Give each process its own database connection, which cannot be shared across a fork.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder, forwarded from --datadir.\n    \"\"\"\n    connect(datadir)\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile', help='output pickle path')\n    ap.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', help='process all sessions (default)')\n    ap.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='process only 2 sessions')\n    ap.add_argument('--workers', type=int, default=10, help='sessions to process in parallel')\n    ap.add_argument('--datadir', default=DATA_DIR,\n                    help='directory holding the one_cache/ folder (default: %(default)s)')\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    connect(args.datadir)\n    selected = select_sessions()\n    pairs = [(eid, subject) for subject, eids in selected.items() for eid in eids]\n    if args.sample:\n        pairs = pairs[:2]\n    print('%d sessions, %d subjects, %d workers'\n          % (len(pairs), len({s for _, s in pairs}), args.workers), flush=True)\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    # sessions are independent, so they are processed out of order and put back by index\n    ordered = [None] * len(pairs)\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers, initializer=worker_setup,\n                             initargs=(args.datadir,)) as pool:\n        futures = {pool.submit(process_session, eid, subject): index\n                   for index, (eid, subject) in enumerate(pairs)}\n\n        progress = tqdm(as_completed(futures), total=len(futures), unit='session')\n        for future in progress:\n            eid, subject = pairs[futures[future]]\n            try:\n                res = future.result()\n            except Exception as error:\n                print('failed %s: %s: %s' % (eid[:8], type(error).__name__, error), flush=True)\n                continue\n\n            # a session with no surviving unit or trial will be skipped\n            if res['n_units'] == 0 or res['n_trials'] < 2:\n                print('skipped %s: %d trials, %d units'\n                      % (res['session_id'][:8], res['n_trials'], res['n_units']), flush=True)\n                continue\n\n            ordered[futures[future]] = res\n            progress.set_postfix_str('%s %s %d trials %d units'\n                                     % (res['session_id'][:8], subject, res['n_trials'], res['n_units']))\n\n    results = [res for res in ordered if res is not None]\n    data = assemble(results)\n    print('\\nconverted in %.1f s' % (time.time() - t0), flush=True)\n    print('sessions %d | subjects %d | regions %d | trials %d | units %d'\n          % (len(results), len(data['subjects']), len(data['brain_regions']),\n             sum(r['n_trials'] for r in results), sum(r['n_units'] for r in results)), flush=True)\n\n    with open(args.outfile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(data, f, protocol=pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL)\n    print('wrote %s (%.1f GB)' % (args.outfile, os.path.getsize(args.outfile) / 1e9), flush=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n","numLines":450,"startLine":1,"totalLines":450}}}

What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number in block*?\n\ni. The 0-based trial index within each block is computed, then **divided by 100** (`TRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE = 100.0`). The computation is done on all trials before filtering, so the block count reflects the animal's true position.\n\nii.\n```python\nTRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE = 100.0\ntib = trial_number_in_block(p_left).astype(np.float32) / TRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE\n# ...\ninp[1] = tib[k]\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies the right-edge convention by matching the reference code's behavior interpolation grid.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time since stimulus onset* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The time axis represents the right edges of the same bins used for neural binning. Each bin covers `[stimOn + TIME_WINDOW[0] + i*BINSIZE, stimOn + TIME_WINDOW[0] + (i+1)*BINSIZE)`, and the time input for that bin is the right edge `stimOn + TIME_WINDOW[0] + (i+1)*BINSIZE`.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Neural bins cover:\nbi = ((spike_times[a:b] - t_beg[k]) / BINSIZE).astype(np.int64)\n# Time input is right edge of the same bins:\ntime_axis = (TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE * np.arange(1, NBINS + 1)).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The time input and neural bins share the same grid structure, differing only in which point within each bin is used as the representative time.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number in block* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `probabilityLeft` in the trials table. A new block starts whenever `probabilityLeft` changes value.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef trial_number_in_block(prob_left):\n    is_new[1:] = ~((prev == cur) | (np.isnan(prev) & np.isnan(cur)))\n    block_start = np.maximum.accumulate(np.where(is_new, np.arange(n), 0))\n    return np.arange(n) - block_start\n```\n\niii. The AI notes the trials table carries no explicit block identifier, so blocks must be recovered from transitions in probabilityLeft.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number in block*?\n\ni. The 0-based trial index within each block is computed, then **divided by 100** (`TRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE = 100.0`). This scaling brings the values to O(1), comparable to the time input. The computation is done on all trials before filtering, so the block count reflects the animal's true position.\n\nii.\n```python\nTRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE = 100.0\ntib = trial_number_in_block(p_left).astype(np.float32) / TRIAL_IN_BLOCK_SCALE\n# ...\ninp[1] = tib[k]\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies the /100 scaling: \"the decoder concatenates inputs with 100 neural PCs and feeds a linear layer with no input standardisation.\" Without scaling, trial_number_in_block can reach ~90, which would dominate the O(1) time input and neural PCs.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Choice* derived from?\n\ni. The `choice` column from the trials table, which takes values +1 (left), -1 (right), or 0 (no response).\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice = trials['choice'].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\nchoice_cls = (choice < 0).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI verified the sign convention: choice == +1 corresponds to a leftward report, verified against contrastLeft/contrastRight and feedbackType.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Choice*?\n\ni. Recoded: +1 (left) -> 0, -1 (right) -> 1. Trials with choice == 0 are excluded by the trial mask.\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice_cls = (choice < 0).astype(np.int64)  # +1 -> 0 (left), -1 -> 1 (right)\n```\n\niii. This matches the decoder task specification: \"left = 0, right = 1\".\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Prior probability of left* derived from?\n\ni. The `probabilityLeft` column from the trials table, which takes values 0.2, 0.5, and 0.8.\n\nii.\n```python\np_left = trials['probabilityLeft'].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\nprior_cls = np.full(len(p_left), -1, dtype=np.int64)\nprior_cls[np.isclose(p_left, 0.2)] = 0\nprior_cls[np.isclose(p_left, 0.5)] = 1\nprior_cls[np.isclose(p_left, 0.8)] = 2\n```\n\niii. The three values correspond to the block structure of the experiment. The AI uses `np.isclose` rather than exact equality for robustness.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Prior probability of left*?\n\ni. Direct mapping: 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2. Trials with unknown probabilityLeft (prior_cls == -1) are dropped.\n\nii.\n```python\nkeep_trial = keep_trial & (prior_cls >= 0)\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task specification.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Wheel speed* derived from?\n\ni. Wheel position and timestamps (`_ibl_wheel.position`, `_ibl_wheel.timestamps`), loaded via `SessionLoader.load_wheel()`. The velocity is computed internally by the loader, and speed is the absolute value.\n\nii.\n```python\nsl.load_wheel()\nwheel_t = sl.wheel['times'].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\nwheel_v = np.abs(sl.wheel['velocity'].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))\n```\n\niii. Same as the reference: `wheel-speed` = `abs(velocity)`.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Wheel speed*?\n\ni. Three steps: (1) `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` internally interpolates position onto a 1000 Hz grid and differentiates with a Butterworth low-pass filter to get velocity; (2) `abs(velocity)` gives speed, which is linearly interpolated onto the **right edges** of the 100 bins of each trial; (3) per-session tertile discretization at the 33.3/66.7 percentiles.\n\nii.\n```python\nws_vals, ws_ok = interp_behavior(wheel_t, wheel_v, t_beg)\n# In interp_behavior:\ngrid = BINSIZE * np.arange(1, NBINS + 1)  # right edges\nxi = t_beg[:, None] + grid[None, :]\nvals = np.interp(safe, times, values).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI follows the reference `get_behavior_per_interval` which evaluates at `np.linspace(beg+binsize, end, n_bins)` = right edges.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Wheel speed* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session tertile bins at the 33.3rd and 66.7th percentiles, computed over all kept time bins of the session. Values are classified as 0 (low), 1 (medium), 2 (high) using `(values > lo) + (values > hi)`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef tertile_bins(values, mask):\n    pool = values[mask].ravel()\n    lo, hi = np.percentile(pool, [100.0 / 3.0, 200.0 / 3.0])\n    cls = (values > lo).astype(np.int64) + (values > hi).astype(np.int64)\n    return cls, (float(lo), float(hi))\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies per-session tertiles: \"Wheel speed and whisker ME are strongly right-skewed and their absolute scale differs by an order of magnitude between sessions... Tertiles give three equally populated, comparable classes.\"\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Wheel speed* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The wheel speed is interpolated onto the same right-edge time grid as the time input, which corresponds to the right edges of the neural bins. Both streams are on the same session clock.\n\nii.\n```python\ngrid = BINSIZE * np.arange(1, NBINS + 1)\nxi = t_beg[:, None] + grid[None, :]\nvals = np.interp(safe, times, values)\n```\n\niii. The motion energy from the side camera: `<side>Camera.ROIMotionEnergy` with `_ibl_<side>Camera.times`. Same left-then-right fallback as the reference.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Whisker motion energy*?\n\ni. The released trace is used as-is (no filtering or normalization). It is linearly interpolated onto the right-edge bin grid, then discretized into per-session tertiles.\n\nii.\n```python\nwm_vals, wm_ok = interp_behavior(whisker_t, whisker_v, t_beg)\nwm_cls, wm_thr = tertile_bins(wm_vals, keep_trial)\n```\n\niii. All behavioral streams are evaluated at the same time points.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Multiple layers of defense: (1) NaN spike times are dropped before sorting/binning; (2) Trials with NaN in critical fields are excluded by the trial mask; (3) Trials not covered by wheel, whisker, or spike data are dropped; (4) Sessions with < 5 well-isolated grey-matter neurons are dropped; (5) Sessions with < 2 usable trials are dropped; (6) Probes with no spike sorting are skipped; (7) A constant-value behavior trace gets a degenerate but valid discretization.\n\nii.\n```python\nfinite = np.isfinite(spk_times)\nspk_times, spk_clusters = spk_times[finite], spk_clusters[finite]\n# ...\nif keep_trial.sum() < 2:\n    return {'eid': eid, 'skip': f'only {int(keep_trial.sum())} usable trials'}\n# ...\nMIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5\n```\n\niii. The AI documented each edge case and fix in CONVERSION_NOTES Step 10. The spike coverage check was added after finding all-zero neural matrices in specific sessions.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. Loading spike sorting from disk, which involves reading large binary files (hundreds of MB per probe). The AI uses `ProcessPoolExecutor` with 24-32 workers for session-level parallelism.\n\nii.\n```python\nssl = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=pname)\nspikes, clusters, channels = ssl.load_spike_sorting()\n```\n\niii. The `bin_spikes_trials` function loops over trials to bin spikes. Each trial uses `searchsorted` to isolate its window and `np.add.at` to accumulate counts. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The ONE client is re-instantiated in each worker process (`get_one()` called once per `load_session`). Each call reads `~/.one/.caches` and creates a new ONE object.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_session(eid, probes, show_processing=False):\n    one = get_one()\n```\n\niii. However, the actual interpolation of discarded trials is wasted work.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
